At home or with friends, I find myself constantly interrupting my family during conversations or butting into my friends' games before they even invite me.\n\nDoctor: Besides interrupting or blurting things out, do you also often feel physically fidgety or unable to stay seated when you’re expected to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience physical fidgetiness or have trouble staying seated when I'm supposed to. My restlessness is much more about blurting things out and interrupting people rather than needing to move my body.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often have several other hyperactive or impulsive behaviors too, like running or climbing when it wasn’t appropriate, difficulty waiting your turn, or acting as if driven by a motor?"
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't recall having those specific behaviors when I was a child. I don't remember running or climbing inappropriately, having trouble waiting my turn, or feeling like I was driven by a motor.

It's making it hard to get through my daily routine, so I wanted to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have these attention and restlessness problems been present since childhood, or did they start more recently during a period of increased worry or unusually high energy with less need for sleep?\n\nPatient: These issues have definitely been with me since childhood, well before I turned 12, so it's not a recent thing caused by worry or a sudden burst of high energy. That said, they have become particularly difficult and noticeable over the last year and nine months.\n\nDoctor: Which causes more problems for you day to day: trouble paying attention and staying organized, or feeling fidgety, unable to sit still, and acting before thinking?\n\nPatient: Honestly, both areas cause major issues for me in my daily life. I really struggle to organize my tasks and often avoid things that require a lot of mental effort, but I also constantly find myself blurting out answers and interrupting or intruding on other people's conversations.\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and impulsivity problems happen in more than one setting, like both at work or school and at home or in social situations?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really thought about it that much. I'm not entirely sure if these issues happen across all those different settings, or if it's just in certain situations.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me one example of these problems at work or school and one example at home or with other people?\n\nPatient: At work, I really struggle to organize my projects and end up actively avoiding any reports or tasks that require long periods of deep mental effort. At home or with friends, I find myself constantly interrupting my family during conversations or butting into my friends' games before they even invite me.\n\nDoctor: Besides interrupting or blurting things out, do you also often feel physically fidgety or unable to stay seated when you’re expected to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience physical fidgetiness or have trouble staying seated when I'm supposed to. My restlessness is much more about blurting things out and interrupting people rather than needing to move my body.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often have several other hyperactive or impulsive behaviors too, like running or climbing when it wasn’t appropriate, difficulty waiting your turn, or acting as if driven by a motor?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having those specific behaviors when I was a child. I don't remember running or climbing inappropriately, having trouble waiting my turn, or feeling like I was driven by a motor.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you also often lose things, get easily distracted, seem not to listen, make careless mistakes, forget daily tasks, or fail to finish schoolwork or chores?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't recall having those specific difficulties when I was a child. I don't remember frequently losing my things, being easily distracted, or forgetting my daily tasks back then.

I'm not entirely sure if these issues happen across all those different settings, or if it's just in certain situations.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me one example of these problems at work or school and one example at home or with other people?\n\nPatient: At work, I really struggle to organize my projects and end up actively avoiding any reports or tasks that require long periods of deep mental effort. At home or with friends, I find myself constantly interrupting my family during conversations or butting into my friends' games before they even invite me.\n\nDoctor: Besides interrupting or blurting things out, do you also often feel physically fidgety or unable to stay seated when you’re expected to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience physical fidgetiness or have trouble staying seated when I'm supposed to. My restlessness is much more about blurting things out and interrupting people rather than needing to move my body.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often have several other hyperactive or impulsive behaviors too, like running or climbing when it wasn’t appropriate, difficulty waiting your turn, or acting as if driven by a motor?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having those specific behaviors when I was a child. I don't remember running or climbing inappropriately, having trouble waiting my turn, or feeling like I was driven by a motor.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you also often lose things, get easily distracted, seem not to listen, make careless mistakes, forget daily tasks, or fail to finish schoolwork or chores?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having those specific difficulties when I was a child. I don't remember frequently losing my things, being easily distracted, or forgetting my daily tasks back then.\n\nDoctor: Right now, besides trouble organizing tasks and avoiding long mental effort, do you often make careless mistakes, lose things, get easily distracted, forget daily tasks, seem not to listen, or leave tasks unfinished?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those other issues. I don't tend to make careless mistakes, lose my things, or get easily distracted, and I usually finish my tasks without forgetting my daily routine.\n\nDoctor: Right now, besides interrupting or intruding on others, do you often have trouble waiting your turn, blurt out answers before a question is finished, or feel driven to keep talking?\n\nPatient: Yes, I frequently find myself blurting out answers before the other person has even finished their question. However, I don't really struggle with waiting my turn in other situations, nor do I feel an unusual drive to keep talking constantly."

Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).
